{"candidates": ["Binge-Eating Disorder", "Major Depressive Disorder"], "note": "The patient's description of recurrent episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, feeling a lack of control during these episodes, and subsequent feelings of guilt and depression align with the criteria for Binge-Eating Disorder. The presence of depressive feelings, especially triggered by the binge eating episodes, keeps Major Depressive Disorder as a consideration, although the patient's depression seems closely tied to the eating episodes rather than being a persistent, independent mood state. The lack of compensatory behaviors (like vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise) rules out other eating disorders like Bulimia Nervosa, which is not on the candidate list anyway. The information gathered so far does not provide sufficient evidence to rule out either of the previous candidates or to introduce new ones, so the candidate list remains unchanged.", "is_final": false}

But I definitely feel incredibly down, guilty, and disgusted with myself immediately after an episode happens.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable or pleasurable that you no longer find interesting or enjoyable, outside of the context of eating or binge episodes?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed losing interest in other activities outside of my eating issues. I'm not really sure about that, since most of my distress is just focused on the episodes themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is heavily influenced by your weight, shape, or eating habits, or is it more related to other aspects of your life?"
  }

I'm not really sure, and I haven't thought about my self-worth in terms of my weight or shape. But I definitely feel very disgusted with myself and incredibly guilty right after my overeating episodes.

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation. Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists. Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.
